=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a grid S with H rows and W columns consisting of 1, 2, 3, and ?. The character at the i-th row and j-th column is S_{i,j}.
By replacing each ? in S with 1, 2, or 3, we can obtain 3^q different grids, where q is the number of ?. Among these grids, how many satisfy the following condition? Print the count modulo 998244353.

- Any two adjacent (edge-sharing) cells contain different digits.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
H W
S_{1,1}S_{1,2}\ldots S_{1,W}
S_{2,1}S_{2,2}\ldots S_{2,W}
\vdots
S_{H,1}S_{H,2}\ldots S_{H,W}

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1 \leq H, W
- H \times W \leq 200
- H and W are integers.
- S is a grid with H rows and W columns consisting of 1, 2, 3, and ?.

Sample Input 1

2 2
1?
??

Sample Output 1

6

Among the grids obtained by replacing each ? in S with 1, 2, or 3, the following six grids satisfy the condition.
12  12  12  13  13  13
21  23  31  21  31  32

Sample Input 2

2 3
123
3?1

Sample Output 2

0

None of the grids obtained by replacing ? satisfies the condition.

Sample Input 3

8 8
3?1?????
???1????
??????2?
????????
????????
????13??
??13?1??
????????

Sample Output 3

779135038

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

dp = {initial_state: 1}

for i in range(n):
    row = grid[i]
    for j in range(m):
        fixed = -1
        ch = row[j]
        if ch != '?': fixed = ord(ch)-49
        sh = shift[j]
        clear = clear_mask[j]
        ndp = {}
        # local variables for speed
        for state, val in dp.items():
            above = (state >> sh) & 3
            # left = (state >> shift[j-1]) & 3 if j>0 else -1
            if j == 0:
                # no left
                for x in (0,1,2):
                    if fixed != -1 and x != fixed: continue
                    if x == above: continue  # above sentinel 3 or previous color
                    ns = (state & clear) | (x << sh)
                    ndp[ns] = (ndp.get(ns,0) + val) % MOD
            else:
                left_shift = shift[j-1]
                left = (state >> left_shift) & 3
                for x in (0,1,2):
                    if fixed != -1 and x != fixed: continue
                    if x == above or x == left: continue
                    ns = (state & clear) | (x << sh)
                    ndp[ns] = (ndp.get(ns,0) + val) % MOD
        dp = ndp
        # maybe prune? no.

Now, let's think about final code structure:
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data: return
    H = int(data[0]); W = int(data[1])
    S = data[2:2+H]
    if H <= W:
        grid = [''.join(S[i][j] for i in range(H)) for j in range(W)]
        n = W
        m = H
    else:
        grid = S[:]
        n = H
        m = W

    MOD = 998244353
    shift = [2*i for i in range(m)]
    clear_mask = [~(3 << sh) for sh in shift]
    bits_by_shift = [[0, 1 << sh, 2 << sh] for sh in shift]  # or tuple
    allowed_flat = [()] * 64
    for above in range(4):
        for left in range(4):
            for fidx in range(4):
                if fidx == 0:
                    arr = tuple(x for x in range(3) if x != above and x != left)
                else:
                    x = fidx - 1
                    if x != above and x != left:
                        arr = (x,)
                    else:
                        arr = ()
                allowed_flat[(above << 4) | (left << 2) | fidx] = arr

    initial_state = 0
    for sh in shift:
        initial_state |= 3 << sh

    dp = {initial_state: 1}
    for i in range(n):
        row = grid[i]
        for j in range(m):
            ch = row[j]
            if ch == '?':
                fidx = 0
            else:
                fidx = ord(ch) - 48
            sh = shift[j]
            clear = clear_mask[j]
            bits = bits_by_shift[j]
            if j == 0:
                left_shift = None
            else:
                left_shift = shift[j-1]
            ndp = {}
            get = ndp.get
            for state, val in dp.items():
                above = (state >> sh) & 3
                if j == 0:
                    left = 3
                else:
                    left = (state >> left_shift) & 3
                allowed = allowed_flat[(above << 4) | (left << 2) | fidx]
                if not allowed:
                    continue
                base = state & clear
                for x in allowed:
                    ns = base | bits[x]
                    old = get(ns)
                    if old is None:
                        ndp[ns] = val
                    else:
                        old += val
                        if old >= MOD:
                            old -= MOD
                        if old:
                            ndp[ns] = old
                        else:
                            del ndp[ns]
            dp = ndp
            if not dp:
                print(0)
                return
    print(sum(dp.values()) % MOD)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Now, let's think about alternative row-state DP if profile DP has a subtle bug with fixed cells and transposition? Let's prove profile DP invariant.

Invariant before processing cell (i,j):
dp maps each possible coloring of the "frontier" cells to number of ways to color all processed cells (rows < i plus row i columns < j) such that all adjacency constraints among processed cells and with frontier? The frontier consists of:
- For columns < j: cell (i, col) (current row processed)
- For columns >= j: cell (i-1, col) (previous row unprocessed)
For i=0, previous row is virtual sentinel 3.
The state stores colors of these frontier cells. Processed cells are all cells above frontier? More precisely, all cells in rows < i-1 and row i-1 columns < j? Wait standard. But the DP transitions ensure when we assign cell (i,j), we check vertical with above (i-1,j) which is in frontier, and horizontal with left (i,j-1) which is in frontier. After assignment, cell (i,j) becomes frontier; above cell (i-1,j) is no longer needed because all future cells that need it? Future cell (i+1,j) will need color of (i,j), not (i-1,j). So overwrite. This is correct. Processed cells include all cells above row i and row i columns < j. The frontier separates processed from unprocessed. The constraints among processed cells have been enforced when each was assigned. The only constraints involving unprocessed cells are with frontier, enforced later. Thus DP counts valid partial colorings. Good.

Wait new coordinates (row=j, col=i). Moving down in new (j+1,i) corresponds original (i,j+1)? Let's be careful. Transpose matrix A^T_{j,i}=A_{i,j}. New row index = original column, new col = original row. New vertical neighbor (j+1,i) = original (i,j+1), which is original horizontal neighbor. New horizontal neighbor (j,i+1) = original (i+1,j), original vertical neighbor. So axes swapped. But condition symmetric. Good.

Actually first row of transposed corresponds original column 0; horizontal constraints within that row (new horizontal) correspond original vertical constraints between rows in same column. The DP enforces horizontal constraints within each new row, so it enforces original vertical constraints. Good. Vertical constraints between new rows correspond original horizontal constraints. The DP enforces vertical constraints between new rows, so original horizontal. Good. Count invariant. Good.

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return

    H = int(data[0])
    W = int(data[1])
    S = data[2:2 + H]

    # Use the smaller dimension as the DP width.
    if H <= W:
        grid = [''.join(S[i][j] for i in range(H)) for j in range(W)]
        n, m = W, H
    else:
        grid = S[:]
        n, m = H, W

    MOD = 998244353

    # 2-bit fields: 0,1,2 = digits 1,2,3; 3 = virtual sentinel
    shift = [2 * i for i in range(m)]
    clear_mask = [~(3 << sh) for sh in shift]
    bits_by_shift = [[0, 1 << sh, 2 << sh] for sh in shift]

    # allowed_flat[above][left][fixed_index]
    # fixed_index: 0 = '?', 1/2/3 = fixed digit 1/2/3
    allowed_flat = [()] * 64
    for above in range(4):
        for left in range(4):
            for fidx in range(4):
                if fidx == 0:
                    arr = tuple(x for x in range(3) if x != above and x != left)
                else:
                    x = fidx - 1
                    arr = (x,) if x != above and x != left else ()
                allowed_flat[(above << 4) | (left << 2) | fidx] = arr

    initial_state = 0
    for sh in shift:
        initial_state |= 3 << sh

    dp = {initial_state: 1}

    for i in range(n):
        row = grid[i]
        for j in range(m):
            ch = row[j]
            if ch == '?':
                fidx = 0
            else:
                fidx = ord(ch) - 48  # '1'->1, '2'->2, '3'->3

            sh = shift[j]
            clear = clear_mask[j]
            bits = bits_by_shift[j]

            if j == 0:
                left_shift = None
            else:
                left_shift = shift[j - 1]

            ndp = {}
            get = ndp.get

            for state, val in dp.items():
                above = (state >> sh) & 3

                if j == 0:
                    left = 3
                else:
                    left = (state >> left_shift) & 3

                allowed = allowed_flat[(above << 4) | (left << 2) | fidx]
                if not allowed:
                    continue

                base = state & clear
                for x in allowed:
                    ns = base | bits[x]
                    old = get(ns)
                    if old is None:
                        ndp[ns] = val
                    else:
                        old += val
                        if old >= MOD:
                            old -= MOD
                        if old:
                            ndp[ns] = old
                        else:
                            del ndp[ns]

            dp = ndp
            if not dp:
                print(0)
                return

    print(sum(dp.values()) % MOD)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
